            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/kilimanjaro20.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/kilimanjaro21.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/kilimanjaro22.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/kilimanjaro23.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/kilimanjaro24.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/kilimanjaro25.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="kilimanjaroText">Located inside the Bomonti Historic Brewery, Kilimanjaro is a casual restaurant bar that is an integral part of Bomontiada. The project received the Time Out Istanbul “Best Restaurant” award. The bar concept was developed and the geometry translated into developable surfaces, defined by zero Gaussian curvature and the ability to be fabricated from flat materials without stretching. Structural drawings were produced, and in addition, detailed static calculations were carried out to ensure structural safety and performance. Based on the engineering data obtained, the correct structural profiles were selected and implemented accordingly. Wall mounted and pendant lighting elements were designed, including preparation of production drawings and supervision of fabrication. The structure was fabricated on site by shipyard craftsmen, reflecting the close relationship between developable surface logic and shipbuilding techniques. On site support during construction included guidance on structural alignment, correct placement, and precise assembly of all components.</p>

            <span class="render-note fade-text" id="kilimanjaroNote">Photography: Engin Aydeniz</span>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

The light leaking from the frames was controlled within a defined mapping system, where the scale of the circular pattern on the printed surface was parametrically adjusted through software.</p>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projOdakuleTitle">EXTERIOR: ODAKULE PASSAGE CONCEPT</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/odakule1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/odakule2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/odakule3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/odakule4.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/odakule5.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/odakule6.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/odakule7.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="odakuleText">Odakule is a key urban passage with heavy pedestrian flow throughout the day, bringing together diverse user groups. The project combines the idea of movement and stepping inherent in the concept of a passage with the production-oriented vision of İstanbul Sanayi Odası, aiming to generate energy from human motion. A piezoelectric flooring system converts each step into kinetic energy. With high daily foot traffic, this creates a meaningful and continuous energy source. The generated energy powers a helical interactive lighting element, establishing a real-time connection between the user and the space. This project was developed in collaboration with Enrico Şansal for the Odakule Passage National Design Competition. As a result, the passage transforms from a simple transit area into an interactive, energy-producing urban experience.</p>

            <a href="https://www.normal.com.tr/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">NORMAL.COM.TR</a>

        </div>

    </div>

    <div class="divider filter-exterior"></div>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projBabajiTitle">FURNITURE-LIGHTING: BABAJI RESTAURANT LIGHTING & FURNITURE DESIGN (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/babaji1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/babaji2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/babaji7.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji9.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/babaji10.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji11.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji12.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji13.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projBabajiText">Babaji, a Soho restaurant by London-based restaurateur Alan Yau, presents a contemporary interpretation of traditional Turkish cuisine and design. The interior draws on Turkey’s rich artisan heritage, featuring handcrafted materials and references to nearly forgotten craft techniques. The restaurant is set within a late 19th-century brick building, with a modest exterior that blends into the vibrant Soho streetscape. Inside, vivid blue ceramic tiles define the space, complemented by traditional Iznik tiles and patterns applied across surfaces and soft furnishings. Custom-designed furniture combines simplicity with refined detailing, including brass inlays inspired by Turkish craftsmanship, reimagined in a modern dotted motif. Spanning three levels, Babaji centers around a striking white cylindrical pide oven on the ground floor, offering a visual and culinary focal point. Additional dining areas are located on the first floor and in the basement, creating a layered and immersive dining experience. As part of a collaborative team effort, I contributed to this project through the design of furniture and lighting fixtures.</p>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/buhais1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="buhaisText">A Venetian blind system was designed to protect the exhibited objects in the museum from UV-related damage. Using GPS-based location data and the sun’s movement, the blinds automatically open and close throughout the day. Additionally, the oculus introduces light into the space, where a vertically moving reflector—functioning like an aperture—controls the diameter of the incoming beam.</p>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projSpiderTitle">LIGHTING: NEW SPIDER WALL LAMP (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/spider1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projSpiderText">Inspired by the form of spiders, Spider is shaped by the circular arrangement of convex rods surrounding a colored bulb. With its striking, sculptural presence, the design stands out as both a functional object and a visual statement. When illuminated, the colored bulb directs light backward and the lamp casts dramatic, oversized shadows onto the surface, creating an unexpected effect that enhances its character. The Spider Wall Lamp project focuses on further developing the design and improving production quality. Additional details have been incorporated to make the product more distinctive and harder to replicate. The collection includes multiple size variations as well as a pendant version.</p>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

    <div class="project-section filter-lighting filter-glassware">

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projCesmiTitle">LIGHTING: ÇEŞM-İ BÜLBÜL WALL LAMP - OMNIA SERIES - PAŞABAHÇE (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a4.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><iframe data-src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/bDlAJAR0JNc" src="" frameborder="0" allowfullscreen></iframe></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projCesmiText">“Çeşm-i Bülbül,” translated as “the eye of the nightingale,” is a traditional Ottoman glassmaking technique developed in the late 18th century. It is distinguished by its intricate, spiraling, multicolored cane (filigree) patterns that resemble the eye of a nightingale. The technique was created by the Mevlevi dervish Mehmed Dede during the reign of Sultan Selim III, drawing inspiration from Venetian glassmaking traditions. This project was developed as an effort to carry this unique art form into the future. It was designed for Paşabahçe’s Omnia special collection and was showcased during the launch event as well as in retail stores.</p>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

            <a href="https://www.pasabahcemagazalari.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">PASABAHCE.COM</a>

        </div>

    </div>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projCapsuleTitle">LIGHTING: CAPSULE WALL LAMP (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/capsule1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/capsule2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projCapsuleText">This wall lamp, defined by its simple circular form, was carefully developed with close attention to proportion. Designed for production using a hydraulic press, all mold development processes were completed in detail. A wide range of color and surface finish variations were explored throughout the design process. The internal lighting element, wiring, and driver were meticulously engineered to ensure both performance and durability. The product was exhibited at Maison&Objet and later sold to CTO Lighting UK.</p>

    <div class="project-section filter-glassware">

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projStoolTitle">GLASSWARE: STOOL - OMNIA SERIES - PAŞABAHÇE (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/gstool1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/gstool2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/gstool3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><iframe data-src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/bDlAJAR0JNc" src="" frameborder="0" allowfullscreen></iframe></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projStoolText">We aimed to take glass beyond its traditional uses and explore it in a new context as a furniture element. This idea led us to consider glass not only as a decorative or functional material, but as a structural and expressive component within furniture design. After developing and testing several prototypes, we refined the concept and arrived at the final product. “Çeşm-i Bülbül,” meaning “the eye of the nightingale,” is a traditional Ottoman glassmaking technique developed in the late 18th century. It is characterized by intricate, spiraling, multicolored cane (filigree) patterns that resemble the eye of a nightingale. The technique was created by the Mevlevi dervish Mehmed Dede during the reign of Sultan Selim III, inspired by Venetian glassmaking traditions. This project was developed as an effort to reinterpret and carry this unique craft into the future. It was designed for Paşabahçe’s Omnia special collection and presented both at the launch event and in retail stores.</p>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

            <a href="https://www.pasabahcemagazalari.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">PASABAHCE.COM</a>

        </div>

    </div>

    <div class="divider filter-glassware"></div>

        <div class="gallery">

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="lavaText">Wabi-Sabi (noun): A concept, an aesthetic, and a worldview. Simply an initiative way of living that emphasizes finding beauty in imperfection, and accepting the natural cycle of growth and decay. The works you will see here are composed of parametric geometries organized with the help of computers, blending the logic of Wabi-sabi and the natural flow of glass. Curves are drawn with the assistance of mathematical equations.</p>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projBronzeText">The REF-funded Má I Bronze Age Boat Project explored Bronze Age maritime technologies through a comprehensive analysis of archaeological, textual, iconographic, and ethnographic data. The project combined conventional research methodologies with indigenous knowledge systems, integrating expert shipwrights from the western Indian Ocean who specialize in traditional boatbuilding techniques. Their practical expertise provided critical insights that extended beyond academic research, enabling a more authentic reconstruction process and allowing comparisons between historical evidence and living traditions. Building on this foundation, the Má II Project focused on the experimental reconstruction of a full-scale Bronze Age reed boat. This initiative is a collaboration between the Dhakira Center for Heritage Studies at NYU Abu Dhabi, Zayed University, and the Abu Dhabi Department of Culture and Tourism. The project aims to deepen understanding of maritime technology and its role in facilitating trade, cultural exchange, and political networks across the Gulf and the western Indian Ocean between 4000 and 2000 years ago. During the Bronze Age, vessels constructed from reeds and wood, sealed with bitumen, navigated trade routes connecting the Fertile Crescent with regions such as Dilmun (Bahrain), Magan (modern BAE and Umman) and Meluhha (Indus Valley). Although no complete shipwrecks from this period have been discovered, extensive archaeological evidence—including cuneiform texts, pottery, iconography, and material remains—has informed the reconstruction of these vessels. The central outcome of the Má II Project was the design and construction of an 18-meter-long ship using historically accurate materials such as reeds, timber, palm fiber cordage, and bitumen. The vessel was built in collaboration with traditional shipwrights from Kerala, whose knowledge of indigenous construction techniques played a vital role in the process. The reconstructed ship offers valuable insights into the structure, durability, cargo capacity, and sailing performance of Bronze Age vessels from the Umm an-Nar period (2600–2000 BCE). As part of the research and development component of the project, I designed a custom photogrammetric 3D scanning system to digitally document the shipbuilding process. This system captured thousands of high-resolution images from multiple angles, enabling precise 3D reconstruction of construction techniques and material assemblies. The aim was to create a detailed digital archive that preserves intangible craftsmanship knowledge while supporting interdisciplinary collaboration. The project also incorporated sustainable architectural strategies during the construction phase. The building environment utilized technical fabric with UV-filtering properties and a geothermal cooling system, achieving effective climate control without reliance on conventional air conditioning. Following the success of the initial reconstruction, a larger vessel was later developed as a continuation of the project. This ship is currently exhibited at the Zayed National Museum and has received significant international media attention, including coverage by National Geographic.</p>

            <a href="https://heritagelab.center/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">HERITAGELAB.CENTER</a>

            <a href="https://www.nationalgeographic.com/travel/article/paid-content-magan-boat-rebuilding-uae-maritime-history" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">NATIONALGEOGRAPHIC.COM</a>

        </div>

    </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projVentilatorText">Reverse engineering and production of a ventilator originally designed by Japanese anesthesiologist Naoyuki Ishikita for use on the ISS, developed in response to potential ventilator shortages during the pandemic. During this period, major Turkish home appliance manufacturers and technology companies, such as Arçelik, Vestel, and Baykar, rapidly initiated efforts to mass-produce mechanical ventilators. As a contingency plan for triaged patients, a task force was established under ETMK (Industrial Designers’ Society of Turkey), bringing together multidisciplinary teams to develop alternative solutions. Following extensive testing and evaluation, the most functional and manufacturable design was selected for further development and potential production.</p>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projAquariumText">These projects showcase a meticulously planned freshwater high-tech planted aquarium that merges advanced aquatic technology with natural aquascaping artistry. The design features a fully planted layout supported by high-intensity LED lighting, pressurized CO₂ injection, and automated nutrient dosing to promote vibrant, healthy plant growth. Careful attention was given to hardscape composition, plant selection, and flow dynamics, resulting in a balanced ecosystem that is both visually striking and biologically stable. Utilizing principles of biomimicry and Voronoi diagrams, I designed and 3D-printed modular structures of varying sizes that resemble mountainous formations. Their perforated surfaces allow water to flow naturally through the structures, mimicking organic currents, while internal cavities are filled with highly porous volcanic rock. These materials support aerobic bacteria on outer surfaces and anaerobic bacteria within inner pores, working together to biologically filter the water by converting toxic ammonia and nitrite into nitrate—an essential nutrient for aquatic plants. Surrounding these printed structures, mosses were carefully attached using transparent fishing line, allowing them to anchor and thrive by utilizing available nutrients. Throughout the process, I conducted experiments with various plant and moss species under different environmental conditions, including pH, KH, GH, conductivity, nutrient levels, CO₂ concentration, lighting duration, and Kelvin values. This integrated system promotes natural plant growth while enhancing biological filtration, resulting in a self-sustaining, bacteria-driven water purification ecosystem.</p>

        </div>

    <script>

        document.querySelectorAll('.gallery').forEach(gallery => {

            let items = Array.from(gallery.children);

            items.sort(() => Math.random() - 0.5);

            items.forEach(item => gallery.appendChild(item));

        });

        const cursorDot = document.getElementById('cursor-dot');

        const cursorOutline = document.getElementById('cursor-outline');

        const cursorTimer = document.getElementById('cursor-timer');

        const cursorCircle = document.getElementById('cursor-timer-circle');

        const cursorText = document.getElementById('cursor-text');

        window.addEventListener('mousemove', function(e) {

            cursorDot.style.left = e.clientX + 'px';

            cursorDot.style.top = e.clientY + 'px';

            cursorOutline.style.transform = `translate(calc(${e.clientX}px - 50%), calc(${e.clientY}px - 50%))`;

            cursorTimer.style.transform = `translate(calc(${e.clientX}px - 50%), calc(${e.clientY}px - 50%))`;

        });

        document.querySelectorAll('.gallery').forEach(gallery => {

            let hoverTimer;

            let hoverTween;

            gallery.addEventListener('mouseenter', () => {

                if (!gallery.classList.contains('expanded')) {

                    cursorText.innerText = currentLang === 'en' ? 'EXPAND' : 'YAYIL';

                    gsap.to(cursorTimer, { opacity: 1, duration: 0.2 });

                    gsap.to(cursorOutline, { opacity: 0, duration: 0.2 });

                    hoverTween = gsap.fromTo(cursorCircle,

                        { strokeDashoffset: 164 },

                        { strokeDashoffset: 0, duration: 3, ease: "none" }

                    );

                    hoverTimer = setTimeout(() => {

                        expandGallery(gallery);

                    }, 3000);

                }

            });

            gallery.addEventListener('mouseleave', () => {

                clearTimeout(hoverTimer);

                if (hoverTween) hoverTween.kill();

                gsap.to(cursorTimer, { opacity: 0, duration: 0.2 });

                gsap.to(cursorOutline, { opacity: 1, duration: 0.2 });

                gsap.set(cursorCircle, { strokeDashoffset: 164 });

            });

            gallery.addEventListener('click', (e) => {

                if (!gallery.classList.contains('expanded')) {

                    expandGallery(gallery);

                }

            });

        });

        document.querySelectorAll('.project-section').forEach(sec => {

            sec.addEventListener('mouseenter', () => {

                document.querySelectorAll('.gallery.expanded').forEach(g => {

                    if (!sec.contains(g)) {

                        g.classList.remove('expanded');

                    }

                });

            });

        });

        function expandGallery(gal) {

            document.querySelectorAll('.gallery.expanded').forEach(g => {

                if (g !== gal) g.classList.remove('expanded');

            });

           

            gal.classList.add('expanded');

           

            gsap.to(cursorTimer, { opacity: 0, duration: 0.2 });

            gsap.to(cursorOutline, { opacity: 1, duration: 0.2 });

            gsap.set(cursorCircle, { strokeDashoffset: 164 });

           

            setTimeout(() => ScrollTrigger.refresh(true), 600);

        }

        document.querySelectorAll('.hoverable, .cat-item, .menu-toggle, .lb-close, .project-link').forEach(el => {

            el.addEventListener('mouseenter', () => {

                cursorOutline.style.width = '60px';

                cursorOutline.style.height = '60px';

                cursorOutline.style.backgroundColor = 'rgba(255,145,175, 0.1)';

            });

            el.addEventListener('mouseleave', () => {

                cursorOutline.style.width = '40px';

                cursorOutline.style.height = '40px';

                cursorOutline.style.backgroundColor = 'transparent';

            });

        });

        const logoElement = document.getElementById('header-logo');

        const fonts = ['Cinzel', 'Playfair Display', 'Cormorant Garamond', 'Montserrat', 'Helvetica'];

        logoElement.innerHTML = "AYTEK JANE".split('').map(char => char === " " ? `<span class="space">&nbsp;</span>` : `<span>${char}</span>`).join('');

        const logoSpans = logoElement.querySelectorAll('span:not(.space)');

        let lastTime = 0;

        function changeLogoFont(timestamp) {

            if (timestamp - lastTime > 800) {

                const randomSpan = logoSpans[Math.floor(Math.random() * logoSpans.length)];

                const randomFont = fonts[Math.floor(Math.random() * fonts.length)];

                randomSpan.style.fontFamily = randomFont;

                lastTime = timestamp;

            }

            requestAnimationFrame(changeLogoFont);

        }

        requestAnimationFrame(changeLogoFont);

        function toggleMobileMenu() {

            const menu = document.getElementById('mobileMenu');

            const body = document.getElementById('page-body');

            menu.classList.toggle('active');

            if (menu.classList.contains('active')) { body.style.overflow = 'hidden'; } else { body.style.overflow = ''; }

        }

                projBronzeTitle: "R&D: BRONZE AGE BOAT, 3D SCANNER, MUSEUM (IN-HOUSE WORK: NEW YORK UNIVERSITY ABU DHABI - DESIGN ENGINEER)",

                projBronzeText: "The REF-funded Má I Bronze Age Boat Project explored Bronze Age maritime technologies through a comprehensive analysis of archaeological, textual, iconographic, and ethnographic data. The project combined conventional research methodologies with indigenous knowledge systems, integrating expert shipwrights from the western Indian Ocean who specialize in traditional boatbuilding techniques. Their practical expertise provided critical insights that extended beyond academic research, enabling a more authentic reconstruction process and allowing comparisons between historical evidence and living traditions. Building on this foundation, the Má II Project focused on the experimental reconstruction of a full-scale Bronze Age reed boat. This initiative is a collaboration between the Dhakira Center for Heritage Studies at NYU Abu Dhabi, Zayed University, and the Abu Dhabi Department of Culture and Tourism. The project aims to deepen understanding of maritime technology and its role in facilitating trade, cultural exchange, and political networks across the Gulf and the western Indian Ocean between 4000 and 2000 years ago. During the Bronze Age, vessels constructed from reeds and wood, sealed with bitumen, navigated trade routes connecting the Fertile Crescent with regions such as Dilmun (Bahrain), Magan (modern BAE and Umman) and Meluhha (Indus Valley). Although no complete shipwrecks from this period have been discovered, extensive archaeological evidence—including cuneiform texts, pottery, iconography, and material remains—has informed the reconstruction of these vessels. The central outcome of the Má II Project was the design and construction of an 18-meter-long ship using historically accurate materials such as reeds, timber, palm fiber cordage, and bitumen. The vessel was built in collaboration with traditional shipwrights from Kerala, whose knowledge of indigenous construction techniques played a vital role in the process. The reconstructed ship offers valuable insights into the structure, durability, cargo capacity, and sailing performance of Bronze Age vessels from the Umm an-Nar period (2600–2000 BCE). As part of the research and development component of the project, I designed a custom photogrammetric 3D scanning system to digitally document the shipbuilding process. This system captured thousands of high-resolution images from multiple angles, enabling precise 3D reconstruction of construction techniques and material assemblies. The aim was to create a detailed digital archive that preserves intangible craftsmanship knowledge while supporting interdisciplinary collaboration. The project also incorporated sustainable architectural strategies during the construction phase. The building environment utilized technical fabric with UV-filtering properties and a geothermal cooling system, achieving effective climate control without reliance on conventional air conditioning. Following the success of the initial reconstruction, a larger vessel was later developed as a continuation of the project. This ship is currently exhibited at the Zayed National Museum and has received significant international media attention, including coverage by National Geographic.",

                projVentilatorTitle: "R&D: VENTILATION DEVICE",

                projVentilatorText: "Reverse engineering and production of a ventilator originally designed by Japanese anesthesiologist Naoyuki Ishikita for use on the ISS, developed in response to potential ventilator shortages during the pandemic. During this period, major Turkish home appliance manufacturers and technology companies, such as Arçelik, Vestel, and Baykar, rapidly initiated efforts to mass-produce mechanical ventilators. As a contingency plan for triaged patients, a task force was established under ETMK (Industrial Designers’ Society of Turkey), bringing together multidisciplinary teams to develop alternative solutions. Following extensive testing and evaluation, the most functional and manufacturable design was selected for further development and potential production.",

                projAquariumTitle: "HOBBY: FRESHWATER HIGH-TECH PLANTED AQUARIUM DESIGN",

                projAquariumText: "These projects showcase a meticulously planned freshwater high-tech planted aquarium that merges advanced aquatic technology with natural aquascaping artistry. The design features a fully planted layout supported by high-intensity LED lighting, pressurized CO₂ injection, and automated nutrient dosing to promote vibrant, healthy plant growth. Careful attention was given to hardscape composition, plant selection, and flow dynamics, resulting in a balanced ecosystem that is both visually striking and biologically stable. Utilizing principles of biomimicry and Voronoi diagrams, I designed and 3D-printed modular structures of varying sizes that resemble mountainous formations. Their perforated surfaces allow water to flow naturally through the structures, mimicking organic currents, while internal cavities are filled with highly porous volcanic rock. These materials support aerobic bacteria on outer surfaces and anaerobic bacteria within inner pores, working together to biologically filter the water by converting toxic ammonia and nitrite into nitrate—an essential nutrient for aquatic plants. Surrounding these printed structures, mosses were carefully attached using transparent fishing line, allowing them to anchor and thrive by utilizing available nutrients. Throughout the process, I conducted experiments with various plant and moss species under different environmental conditions, including pH, KH, GH, conductivity, nutrient levels, CO₂ concentration, lighting duration, and Kelvin values. This integrated system promotes natural plant growth while enhancing biological filtration, resulting in a self-sustaining, bacteria-driven water purification ecosystem."

                projBronzeText: "REF tarafından finanse edilen Má I Tunç Çağı Gemisi Projesi, arkeolojik, metinsel, ikonografik ve etnografik verilerin kapsamlı bir analizi yoluyla Tunç Çağı denizcilik teknolojilerini araştırmıştır. Proje, geleneksel araştırma metodolojilerini yerel bilgi sistemleriyle birleştirerek, geleneksel tekne yapım tekniklerinde uzmanlaşmış Batı Hint Okyanusu'ndan gemi yapım ustalarını sürece dahil etmiştir. Ustaların pratik uzmanlığı, akademik araştırmaların ötesine geçen kritik bilgiler sağlayarak daha otantik bir yeniden inşa sürecine olanak tanımış ve tarihsel kanıtlarla yaşayan gelenekler arasında karşılaştırmalar yapılmasını sağlamıştır. Bu temel üzerine inşa edilen Má II Projesi, tam ölçekli bir Tunç Çağı kamış gemisinin deneysel olarak yeniden inşasına odaklanmıştır. Bu girişim, NYU Abu Dhabi bünyesindeki Dhakira Miras Çalışmaları Merkezi, Zayed Üniversitesi ve Abu Dabi Kültür ve Turizm Bakanlığı arasındaki bir iş birliğidir. Proje, denizcilik teknolojisinin 4000 ile 2000 yıl önce Körfez ve Batı Hint Okyanusu genelinde ticaret, kültürel değişim ve siyasi ağları kolaylaştırmadaki rolünü daha derinlemesine anlamayı hedeflemektedir. Tunç Çağı boyunca, kamış ve ahşaptan inşa edilen ve bitümle sızdırmaz hale getirilen gemiler, Bereketli Hilal'i Dilmun (Bahreyn), Magan (modern BAE and Umman) ve Meluhha (Indus Vadisi) gibi bölgelere bağlayan ticaret rotalarında seyretmiştir. Bu döneye ait tam bir gemi enkazı keşfedilmemiş olsa da, çivi yazılı metinler, çanak çömlekler, ikonografi ve maddi kalıntılar dahil olmak üzere kapsamlı arkeolojik kanıtlar bu gemilerin yeniden inşasına temel oluşturmuştur. Má II Projesi'nin temel çıktısı, kamış, kereste, palmiye palmiye lifi halat ve bitüm gibi tarihsel olarak doğru malzemeler kullanılarak 18 metre uzunluğunda bir geminin tasarlanması ve inşa edilmesidir. Gemi, yerel inşa teknikleri konusundaki bilgileri süreçte hayati bir rol oynayan Keralalı geleneksel gemi yapım ustalarıyla iş birliği içinde inşa edilmiştir. Yeniden inşa edilen gemi, Umm an-Nar döneminden (M.Ö. 2600–2000) kalma Tunç Çağı gemilerinin yapısı, dayanıklılığı, kargo kapasitesi ve seyir performansı hakkında değerli bilgiler sunmaktadır. Projenin araştırma ve geliştirme bileşeninin bir parçası olarak, gemi yapım sürecini dijital olarak belgelemek için özel bir fotogrammetrik 3B tarama sistemi tasarladım. Bu sistem, birden fazla açıdan binlerce yüksek çözünürlüklü görüntü yakalayarak inşa tekniklerinin ve malzeme birleşimlerinin hassas 3B rekonstrüksiyonuna olanak sağlamıştır. Amaç, disiplinler arası iş birliğini desteklerken somut olmayan zanaat bilgisini koruyan ayrıntılı bir dijital arşiv oluşturmaktı. Proje ayrıca inşa aşamasında sürdürülebilir mimari stratejileri de içermiştir. İnşa ortamı, UV filtreleme özelliklerine sahip teknik kumaş ve jeotermal soğutma sistemi kullanarak, geleneksel klimaya ihtiyaç duymadan etkili bir iklim kontrolü sağlamıştır. İlk yeniden inşanın başarısının ardından, projenin devamı olarak daha büyük bir gemi geliştirilmiştir. Bu gemi şu anda Zayed Ulusal Müzesi'nde sergilenmektedir ve National Geographic dahil olmak üzere uluslararası medyada geniş yer bulmuştur.",

Tasarım, canlı ve sağlıklı bitki büyümesini teşvik etmek için yüksek yoğunluklu LED aydınlatma, basınçlı CO₂ enjeksiyonu ve otomatik besin dozajı ile desteklenen tamamen bitkili bir düzene sahiptir. Hardscape (sert peyzaj) kompozisyonuna, bitki seçimine ve akış dinamiklerine dikkat edilerek hem görsel olarak çarpıcı hem de biyolojik olarak kararlı, dengeli bir ekosistem elde edilmiştir. Biyomimikri ve Voronoi diyagramları prensiplerini kullanarak, dağlık oluşumlara benzeyen çeşitli boyutlarda modüler yapılar tasarladım ve 3B yazıcı ile ürettim. Delikli yüzeyleri, suyun yapılar arasından doğal bir şekilde akmasına izin vererek organik akıntıları taklit ederken, iç boşluklar oldukça gözenekli volkanik kayalarla doldurulmuştur. Bu malzemeler, dış yüzeylerde aerobik (oksijenli) bakterileri ve iç gözeneklerde anaerobik (oksijensiz) bakterileri destekleyerek, toksik amonyak ve nitriti su bitkileri için temel bir besin olan nitrata dönüştürmek suretiyle suyu biyolojik olarak filtrelemek üzere birlikte çalışırlar. Bu basılı yapıların etrafına, şeffaf misina kullanılarak yosunlar dikkatlice tutturulmuş, böylece mevcut besinleri kullanarak tutunmaları ve gelişmeleri sağlanmıştır. Süreç boyunca, pH, KH, GH, iletkenlik, besin seviyeleri, CO₂ konsantrasyonu, aydınlatma süresi ve Kelvin değerleri dahil olmak üzere farklı çevresel koşullar altında çeşitli bitki ve yosun türleri ile deneyler yaptım. Bu entegre sistem, biyolojik filtrasyonu artırırken doğal bitki büyümesini teşvik eder ve sonuç olarak kendi kendini idame ettiren, bakteri odaklı bir su arıtma ekosistemi yaratır."

            }

        };

        let currentLang = 'en';

        function toggleLanguage() {

            currentLang = (currentLang === 'en' ? 'tr' : 'en');

            document.documentElement.lang = currentLang;

           

            Object.keys(data[currentLang]).forEach(id => {

                const el = document.getElementById(id);

                if (el) {

                    const newText = data[currentLang][id];

                    el.dataset.originalText = newText;

                    if (gsap.getProperty(el, "opacity") === 1) {

                        gsap.to(el, { opacity: 0, filter: "blur(5px)", duration: 0.3, onComplete: () => {

                            el.innerHTML = newText;

                            gsap.to(el, { opacity: 1, filter: "blur(0px)", duration: 0.5 });

                        }});

                    } else {

                        el.innerHTML = newText;

                    }

                }

            });

            const btns = ['langBtn', 'langBtnMobile'];

            btns.forEach(id => {

                const b = document.getElementById(id);

                if (b) b.innerText = (currentLang === 'en' ? 'TÜRKÇE' : 'ENGLISH');

            });

            document.querySelectorAll('.cat-item').forEach(item => {

                const map = {

                    tr: {all:'HEPSİ', interior:'İÇ MİMARİ', exterior:'DIŞ MİMARİ', furniture:'MOBİLYA', lighting:'AYDINLATMA', glassware:'CAM EŞYA', accessory:'AKSESUAR', fashion:'MODA', rd:'AR-GE', hobby:'HOBİ'},

                    en: {all:'ALL', interior:'INTERIOR', exterior:'EXTERIOR', furniture:'FURNITURE', lighting:'LIGHTING', glassware:'GLASSWARE', accessory:'ACCESSORY', fashion:'FASHION', rd:'R&D', hobby:'HOBBY'}

                };

                const key = item.id.replace('cat-', '');

                if (map[currentLang][key]) item.innerText = map[currentLang][key];

            });

        }

        function filterProjects(category, element) {

            document.querySelectorAll('.cat-item').forEach(item => item.classList.remove('active'));

            if(element) element.classList.add('active');

           

            lenis.scrollTo(0, { immediate: true });

           

            const introSec = document.getElementById('intro-section');

            if (category === 'all') {

                introSec.style.display = 'flex';

                gsap.fromTo(introSec, { opacity: 0, y: 20 }, { opacity: 1, y: 0, duration: 0.8 });

            } else {

                introSec.style.display = 'none';

            }

            const sections = document.querySelectorAll('.project-section');

           

            let firstVisible = false;

           

            sections.forEach(sec => {

                if (category === 'all' || sec.classList.contains('filter-' + category)) {

                    sec.style.display = 'block';

                   

                    if (!firstVisible && category !== 'all') {

                        sec.style.marginTop = '200px';

                        firstVisible = true;

                    } else {

                        sec.style.marginTop = '0px';

                    }

                    gsap.set(sec, { opacity: 1, y: 0 });

                } else {

                    sec.style.display = 'none';

                    sec.style.marginTop = '0px';

                }

            });

           

            let visibleSections = Array.from(sections).filter(sec => sec.style.display === 'block');

            document.querySelectorAll('.divider').forEach(div => div.style.display = 'none');

            visibleSections.forEach((sec, index) => {

                if (index < visibleSections.length - 1) {

                    let nextEl = sec.nextElementSibling;

                    if(nextEl && nextEl.classList.contains('divider')){

                        nextEl.style.display = 'block';

                    }

                }

            });

           

            const menu = document.getElementById('mobileMenu');

            if(window.innerWidth <= 768 && menu) {

                menu.classList.remove('active');

                document.body.style.overflow = '';

            }

           

            setTimeout(() => { ScrollTrigger.refresh(true); }, 100);

        }

        let currentVisibleImages = [];

        let currentImageIndex = 0;

        function openLightbox(img) {

            if (!img.closest('.gallery').classList.contains('expanded')) return;

            const visibleSections = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('.project-section')).filter(sec => sec.style.display !== 'none');

            currentVisibleImages = [];

            visibleSections.forEach(sec => {

                const imgs = sec.querySelectorAll('.img-wrapper img');

                imgs.forEach(i => currentVisibleImages.push(i));

            });

            currentImageIndex = currentVisibleImages.findIndex(i => i.src === img.src || i.dataset.src === img.dataset.src);

            if (currentImageIndex === -1) currentImageIndex = 0;

            const lightboxImg = document.getElementById('lightboxImg');

            lightboxImg.src = img.src || img.dataset.src;

           

            document.getElementById('lightbox').style.display = 'flex';

            document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden';

        }

        function nextLightboxImage(event) {

            if (event) event.stopPropagation();

            if (currentVisibleImages.length === 0) return;

           

            currentImageIndex = (currentImageIndex + 1) % currentVisibleImages.length;

            const lightboxImg = document.getElementById('lightboxImg');

           

            gsap.fromTo(lightboxImg, {opacity: 0.5}, {opacity: 1, duration: 0.3});

            lightboxImg.src = currentVisibleImages[currentImageIndex].src || currentVisibleImages[currentImageIndex].dataset.src;

        }

        function closeLightbox(event) {

            if (event && event.target.id === 'lightboxImg') return;

            document.getElementById('lightbox').style.display = 'none';

            document.body.style.overflow = '';

        }

       

        window.addEventListener('keydown', e => {

            if (e.key === "Escape") closeLightbox();

            if (e.key === "ArrowRight" && document.getElementById('lightbox').style.display === 'flex') nextLightboxImage();

        });

        // ==========================================

        // EDİTORYAL ANİMASYON VE EFEKT KODLARI

        // ==========================================

       

        gsap.registerPlugin(ScrollTrigger);

        const lenis = new Lenis({

            duration: 1.2,

            easing: (t) => Math.min(1, 1.001 - Math.pow(2, -10 * t)),

            direction: 'vertical',

            smooth: true,

            smoothTouch: false

        });

        lenis.on('scroll', ScrollTrigger.update);

        gsap.ticker.add((time) => { lenis.raf(time * 1000); });

        gsap.ticker.lagSmoothing(0);

        gsap.utils.toArray('.img-wrapper').forEach((wrapper, i) => {

            const speed = (i % 3 === 0) ? -25 : (i % 3 === 1) ? -45 : -15;

            gsap.to(wrapper, {

                "--parallax-y": speed + "px",

                ease: "none",

                scrollTrigger: {

                    trigger: wrapper.closest('.gallery'),

                    start: "top bottom",

                    end: "bottom top",

                    scrub: true

                }

            });

        });

        gsap.utils.toArray('.project-desc-container').forEach(el => {

            gsap.fromTo(el,

                { y: 60 },

                { y: -60, ease: "none", scrollTrigger: {

                    trigger: el.closest('.project-section'),

                    start: "top bottom",

                    end: "bottom top",

                    scrub: true

                }}

            );

        });

        gsap.utils.toArray('.project-header').forEach(el => {

            gsap.fromTo(el,

                { y: 30 },

                { y: -30, ease: "none", scrollTrigger: {

                    trigger: el.closest('.project-section'),

                    start: "top bottom",

                    end: "bottom top",

                    scrub: true

                }}

            );

        });

        gsap.utils.toArray('.project-link').forEach(el => {

            gsap.fromTo(el,

                { y: 20 },

                { y: -20, ease: "none", scrollTrigger: {

                    trigger: el.closest('.project-section'),

                    start: "top bottom",

                    end: "bottom top",

                    scrub: true

                }}

            );

        });

        function playPixels(wrapper) {

            const pixels = wrapper.querySelectorAll('.pixel-block');

            gsap.fromTo(pixels,

                {opacity: 1},

                {

                    opacity: 0,

                    duration: 0.8,

                    stagger: { amount: 0.7, from: "random" },

                    ease: "power2.inOut",

                    overwrite: true

                }

            );

        }

        function hidePixels(wrapper) {

            const pixels = wrapper.querySelectorAll('.pixel-block');

            gsap.set(pixels, {opacity: 1});

        }

        document.querySelectorAll('.fade-text').forEach(el => {

            el.dataset.originalText = el.innerText;

            gsap.set(el, { opacity: 0, filter: "blur(10px)" });

        });

        document.querySelectorAll('.img-wrapper').forEach(wrapper => {

            const img = wrapper.querySelector('img') || wrapper.querySelector('iframe');

            if(img) {

                let overlay = document.createElement('div');

                overlay.className = 'pixel-overlay';

                for(let i=0; i<64; i++) {

                    let block = document.createElement('div');

                    block.className = 'pixel-block';

                    overlay.appendChild(block);

                }

                wrapper.appendChild(overlay);

            }

        });

        // PROJE ODAKLI LAZY LOAD OBSERVER

        const projectLazyObserver = new IntersectionObserver((entries, observer) => {

            entries.forEach(entry => {

                if (entry.isIntersecting) {

                    const section = entry.target;

                    const mediaEls = section.querySelectorAll('img[data-src], iframe[data-src]');

                   

                    mediaEls.forEach(el => {

                        el.src = el.getAttribute('data-src');

                        el.removeAttribute('data-src');

                    });

                   

                    observer.unobserve(section);

                }

            });

        }, { rootMargin: "1500px 0px" });

        document.querySelectorAll('.project-section').forEach(sec => {

            projectLazyObserver.observe(sec);

        });

        // METİN FLULUĞUNUN SCROLL İLE ÇALIŞMASI (SCROLLTRIGGER)

        gsap.utils.toArray('.fade-text').forEach(el => {

            gsap.to(el, {

                opacity: 1,

                filter: "blur(0px)",

                duration: 1.2,

                ease: "power2.out",

                scrollTrigger: {

                    trigger: el,

                    start: "top 95%", // Element ekrana girmeye başladığında çalıştırır

                    toggleActions: "play none none none" // Yalnızca bir kere çalışsın, geri kaybolmasın

                }

            });

        });

        const revealObserver = new IntersectionObserver((entries, observer) => {

            entries.forEach(entry => {

                if (entry.isIntersecting) {

                    if (entry.target.classList.contains('img-wrapper')) {

                        if (entry.target.dataset.revealed !== 'true') {

                            entry.target.dataset.revealed = 'true';

                            playPixels(entry.target);

                        }

                    }

                } else {

                    if (entry.target.classList.contains('img-wrapper')) {

                        entry.target.dataset.revealed = 'false';

                        hidePixels(entry.target);

                    }

                }

            });

        }, { threshold: 0.05, rootMargin: "0px 0px -5% 0px" });

        document.querySelectorAll('.img-wrapper').forEach(el => revealObserver.observe(el));

        const introSection = document.querySelector('.intro');

        gsap.fromTo(introSection, { y: 40, opacity: 0 }, { y: 0, opacity: 1, duration: 1.5, ease: "power3.out", delay: 0.2 });

       

        filterProjects('all', document.getElementById('cat-all'));

    </script>

</body>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="kilimanjaroText">Located inside the Bomonti Historic Brewery, Kilimanjaro is a casual restaurant bar that is an integral part of Bomontiada. The project received the Time Out Istanbul “Best Restaurant” award. The bar concept was developed and the geometry translated into developable surfaces, defined by zero Gaussian curvature and the ability to be fabricated from flat materials without stretching. Structural drawings were produced, and in addition, detailed static calculations were carried out to ensure structural safety and performance. Based on the engineering data obtained, the correct structural profiles were selected and implemented accordingly. Wall mounted and pendant lighting elements were designed, including preparation of production drawings and supervision of fabrication. The structure was fabricated on site by shipyard craftsmen, reflecting the close relationship between developable surface logic and shipbuilding techniques. On site support during construction included guidance on structural alignment, correct placement, and precise assembly of all components.</p>

            <span class="render-note fade-text" id="kilimanjaroNote">Photography: Engin Aydeniz</span>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="sinemiraText">Conducted on-site measurements of existing yacht interiors to support accurate design development, followed by the preparation of detailed plans and section drawings. The light leaking from the frames was controlled within a defined mapping system, where the scale of the circular pattern on the printed surface was parametrically adjusted through software.</p>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projOdakuleTitle">EXTERIOR: ODAKULE PASSAGE CONCEPT</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/odakule1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/odakule2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/odakule3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/odakule4.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/odakule5.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/odakule6.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/odakule7.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="odakuleText">Odakule is a key urban passage with heavy pedestrian flow throughout the day, bringing together diverse user groups. The project combines the idea of movement and stepping inherent in the concept of a passage with the production-oriented vision of İstanbul Sanayi Odası, aiming to generate energy from human motion. A piezoelectric flooring system converts each step into kinetic energy. With high daily foot traffic, this creates a meaningful and continuous energy source. The generated energy powers a helical interactive lighting element, establishing a real-time connection between the user and the space. This project was developed in collaboration with Enrico Şansal for the Odakule Passage National Design Competition. As a result, the passage transforms from a simple transit area into an interactive, energy-producing urban experience.</p>

            <a href="https://www.normal.com.tr/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">NORMAL.COM.TR</a>

        </div>

    </div>

    <div class="divider filter-exterior"></div>

The interplay between the precise formal structure and the softness of the emitted light creates a composition that balances sharpness with serenity.</p>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projBabajiTitle">FURNITURE-LIGHTING: BABAJI RESTAURANT LIGHTING & FURNITURE DESIGN (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/babaji1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/babaji2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/babaji3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji6.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/babaji7.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji8.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji9.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/babaji10.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji11.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji12.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/babaji13.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projBabajiText">Babaji, a Soho restaurant by London-based restaurateur Alan Yau, presents a contemporary interpretation of traditional Turkish cuisine and design. The interior draws on Turkey’s rich artisan heritage, featuring handcrafted materials and references to nearly forgotten craft techniques. The restaurant is set within a late 19th-century brick building, with a modest exterior that blends into the vibrant Soho streetscape. Inside, vivid blue ceramic tiles define the space, complemented by traditional Iznik tiles and patterns applied across surfaces and soft furnishings. Custom-designed furniture combines simplicity with refined detailing, including brass inlays inspired by Turkish craftsmanship, reimagined in a modern dotted motif. Spanning three levels, Babaji centers around a striking white cylindrical pide oven on the ground floor, offering a visual and culinary focal point. Additional dining areas are located on the first floor and in the basement, creating a layered and immersive dining experience. As part of a collaborative team effort, I contributed to this project through the design of furniture and lighting fixtures.</p>

            <span class="render-note fade-text" id="babajiNote">Photography Ed Reeve</span>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projBuhaisTitle">LIGHTING: BUHAIS GEOLOGY PARK - AUTOMATIC VENETIAN BLIND & OCULUS (IN-HOUSE WORK: JVL)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/buhais1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/buhais2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="buhaisText">A Venetian blind system was designed to protect the exhibited objects in the museum from UV-related damage. Using GPS-based location data and the sun’s movement, the blinds automatically open and close throughout the day. Additionally, the oculus introduces light into the space, where a vertically moving reflector—functioning like an aperture—controls the diameter of the incoming beam.</p>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projSpiderTitle">LIGHTING: NEW SPIDER WALL LAMP (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/spider1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projSpiderText">Inspired by the form of spiders, Spider is shaped by the circular arrangement of convex rods surrounding a colored bulb. With its striking, sculptural presence, the design stands out as both a functional object and a visual statement. When illuminated, the colored bulb directs light backward and the lamp casts dramatic, oversized shadows onto the surface, creating an unexpected effect that enhances its character. The Spider Wall Lamp project focuses on further developing the design and improving production quality. Additional details have been incorporated to make the product more distinctive and harder to replicate. The collection includes multiple size variations as well as a pendant version.</p>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

    <div class="project-section filter-lighting filter-glassware">

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projCesmiTitle">LIGHTING: ÇEŞM-İ BÜLBÜL WALL LAMP - OMNIA SERIES - PAŞABAHÇE (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/walllamp-a4.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><iframe data-src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/bDlAJAR0JNc" src="" frameborder="0" allowfullscreen></iframe></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projCesmiText">“Çeşm-i Bülbül,” translated as “the eye of the nightingale,” is a traditional Ottoman glassmaking technique developed in the late 18th century. It is distinguished by its intricate, spiraling, multicolored cane (filigree) patterns that resemble the eye of a nightingale. The technique was created by the Mevlevi dervish Mehmed Dede during the reign of Sultan Selim III, drawing inspiration from Venetian glassmaking traditions. This project was developed as an effort to carry this unique art form into the future. It was designed for Paşabahçe’s Omnia special collection and was showcased during the launch event as well as in retail stores.</p>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

            <a href="https://www.pasabahcemagazalari.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">PASABAHCE.COM</a>

        </div>

    </div>

    <div class="divider filter-lighting filter-glassware"></div>

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projBurganTitle">LIGHTING: BURGAN BANK PRIVATE - LIGHTING PLAN (IN-HOUSE WORK: I-AM ASSOCIATES)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/burgan1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/burgan2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

    <div class="project-section filter-glassware">

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projStoolTitle">GLASSWARE: STOOL - OMNIA SERIES - PAŞABAHÇE (IN-HOUSE WORK: AUTOBAN)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/gstool1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/gstool2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/gstool3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><iframe data-src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/bDlAJAR0JNc" src="" frameborder="0" allowfullscreen></iframe></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projStoolText">We aimed to take glass beyond its traditional uses and explore it in a new context as a furniture element. This idea led us to consider glass not only as a decorative or functional material, but as a structural and expressive component within furniture design. After developing and testing several prototypes, we refined the concept and arrived at the final product. “Çeşm-i Bülbül,” meaning “the eye of the nightingale,” is a traditional Ottoman glassmaking technique developed in the late 18th century. It is characterized by intricate, spiraling, multicolored cane (filigree) patterns that resemble the eye of a nightingale. The technique was created by the Mevlevi dervish Mehmed Dede during the reign of Sultan Selim III, inspired by Venetian glassmaking traditions. This project was developed as an effort to reinterpret and carry this unique craft into the future. It was designed for Paşabahçe’s Omnia special collection and presented both at the launch event and in retail stores.</p>

            <a href="https://autoban.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">AUTOBAN.COM</a>

            <a href="https://www.pasabahcemagazalari.com/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">PASABAHCE.COM</a>

        </div>

    </div>

    <div class="project-section filter-rd">

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projBronzeTitle">R&D: BRONZE AGE BOAT, 3D SCANNER, MUSEUM (IN-HOUSE WORK: NEW YORK UNIVERSITY ABU DHABI - DESIGN ENGINEER)</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/bronze1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/bronze2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/bronze3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/bronze4.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/bronze5.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/bronze6.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/bronze7.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/bronze8.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/bronze9.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/bronze10.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/bronze11.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/bronze12.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/bronze13.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/bronze14.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/bronze15.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/bronze16.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projBronzeText">The REF-funded Má I Bronze Age Boat Project explored Bronze Age maritime technologies through a comprehensive analysis of archaeological, textual, iconographic, and ethnographic data. The project combined conventional research methodologies with indigenous knowledge systems, integrating expert shipwrights from the western Indian Ocean who specialize in traditional boatbuilding techniques. Their practical expertise provided critical insights that extended beyond academic research, enabling a more authentic reconstruction process and allowing comparisons between historical evidence and living traditions. Building on this foundation, the Má II Project focused on the experimental reconstruction of a full-scale Bronze Age reed boat. This initiative is a collaboration between the Dhakira Center for Heritage Studies at NYU Abu Dhabi, Zayed University, and the Abu Dhabi Department of Culture and Tourism. The project aims to deepen understanding of maritime technology and its role in facilitating trade, cultural exchange, and political networks across the Gulf and the western Indian Ocean between 4000 and 2000 years ago. During the Bronze Age, vessels constructed from reeds and wood, sealed with bitumen, navigated trade routes connecting the Fertile Crescent with regions such as Dilmun (Bahrain), Magan (modern BAE and Umman) and Meluhha (Indus Valley). Although no complete shipwrecks from this period have been discovered, extensive archaeological evidence—including cuneiform texts, pottery, iconography, and material remains—has informed the reconstruction of these vessels. The central outcome of the Má II Project was the design and construction of an 18-meter-long ship using historically accurate materials such as reeds, timber, palm fiber cordage, and bitumen. The vessel was built in collaboration with traditional shipwrights from Kerala, whose knowledge of indigenous construction techniques played a vital role in the process. The reconstructed ship offers valuable insights into the structure, durability, cargo capacity, and sailing performance of Bronze Age vessels from the Umm an-Nar period (2600–2000 BCE). As part of the research and development component of the project, I designed a custom photogrammetric 3D scanning system to digitally document the shipbuilding process. This system captured thousands of high-resolution images from multiple angles, enabling precise 3D reconstruction of construction techniques and material assemblies. The aim was to create a detailed digital archive that preserves intangible craftsmanship knowledge while supporting interdisciplinary collaboration. The project also incorporated sustainable architectural strategies during the construction phase. The building environment utilized technical fabric with UV-filtering properties and a geothermal cooling system, achieving effective climate control without reliance on conventional air conditioning. Following the success of the initial reconstruction, a larger vessel was later developed as a continuation of the project. This ship is currently exhibited at the Zayed National Museum and has received significant international media attention, including coverage by National Geographic.</p>

            <a href="https://heritagelab.center/" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">HERITAGELAB.CENTER</a>

            <a href="https://www.nationalgeographic.com/travel/article/paid-content-magan-boat-rebuilding-uae-maritime-history" target="_blank" class="project-link fade-text">NATIONALGEOGRAPHIC.COM</a>

        </div>

    </div>

    <div class="project-section filter-rd">

        <div class="project-header fade-text" id="projVentilatorTitle">R&D: VENTILATION DEVICE</div>

        <div class="gallery">

            <div class="img-wrapper wide-square"><img data-src="images/vapo1.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper landscape"><img data-src="images/vapo2.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/vapo3.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/vapo4.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper square"><img data-src="images/vapo5.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

            <div class="img-wrapper portrait"><img data-src="images/vapo6.webp" src="" alt="" onclick="openLightbox(this)"></div>

        </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projVentilatorText">Reverse engineering and production of a ventilator originally designed by Japanese anesthesiologist Naoyuki Ishikita for use on the ISS, developed in response to potential ventilator shortages during the pandemic. During this period, major Turkish home appliance manufacturers and technology companies, such as Arçelik, Vestel, and Baykar, rapidly initiated efforts to mass-produce mechanical ventilators. As a contingency plan for triaged patients, a task force was established under ETMK (Industrial Designers’ Society of Turkey), bringing together multidisciplinary teams to develop alternative solutions. Following extensive testing and evaluation, the most functional and manufacturable design was selected for further development and potential production.</p>

        </div>

    </div>

        <div class="project-desc-container">

            <p class="project-desc fade-text" id="projAquariumText">These projects showcase a meticulously planned freshwater high-tech planted aquarium that merges advanced aquatic technology with natural aquascaping artistry. The design features a fully planted layout supported by high-intensity LED lighting, pressurized CO₂ injection, and automated nutrient dosing to promote vibrant, healthy plant growth. Careful attention was given to hardscape composition, plant selection, and flow dynamics, resulting in a balanced ecosystem that is both visually striking and biologically stable. Utilizing principles of biomimicry and Voronoi diagrams, I designed and 3D-printed modular structures of varying sizes that resemble mountainous formations. Their perforated surfaces allow water to flow naturally through the structures, mimicking organic currents, while internal cavities are filled with highly porous volcanic rock. These materials support aerobic bacteria on outer surfaces and anaerobic bacteria within inner pores, working together to biologically filter the water by converting toxic ammonia and nitrite into nitrate—an essential nutrient for aquatic plants. Surrounding these printed structures, mosses were carefully attached using transparent fishing line, allowing them to anchor and thrive by utilizing available nutrients. Throughout the process, I conducted experiments with various plant and moss species under different environmental conditions, including pH, KH, GH, conductivity, nutrient levels, CO₂ concentration, lighting duration, and Kelvin values. This integrated system promotes natural plant growth while enhancing biological filtration, resulting in a self-sustaining, bacteria-driven water purification ecosystem.</p>

        </div>

    </div>

    <script>

        document.querySelectorAll('.gallery').forEach(gallery => {

            let items = Array.from(gallery.children);

            items.sort(() => Math.random() - 0.5);

            items.forEach(item => gallery.appendChild(item));

        });

        const cursorDot = document.getElementById('cursor-dot');

        const cursorOutline = document.getElementById('cursor-outline');

        const cursorTimer = document.getElementById('cursor-timer');

        const cursorCircle = document.getElementById('cursor-timer-circle');

        const cursorText = document.getElementById('cursor-text');

        window.addEventListener('mousemove', function(e) {

            cursorDot.style.left = e.clientX + 'px';

            cursorDot.style.top = e.clientY + 'px';

            cursorOutline.style.transform = `translate(calc(${e.clientX}px - 50%), calc(${e.clientY}px - 50%))`;

            cursorTimer.style.transform = `translate(calc(${e.clientX}px - 50%), calc(${e.clientY}px - 50%))`;

        });

        document.querySelectorAll('.gallery').forEach(gallery => {

            let hoverTimer;

            let hoverTween;

            gallery.addEventListener('mouseenter', () => {

                if (!gallery.classList.contains('expanded')) {

                    cursorText.innerText = currentLang === 'en' ? 'EXPAND' : 'YAYIL';

                    gsap.to(cursorTimer, { opacity: 1, duration: 0.2 });

                    gsap.to(cursorOutline, { opacity: 0, duration: 0.2 });

                    hoverTween = gsap.fromTo(cursorCircle,

                        { strokeDashoffset: 164 },

                        { strokeDashoffset: 0, duration: 3, ease: "none" }

                    );

                    hoverTimer = setTimeout(() => {

                        expandGallery(gallery);

                    }, 3000);

                }

            });

            gallery.addEventListener('mouseleave', () => {

                clearTimeout(hoverTimer);

                if (hoverTween) hoverTween.kill();

                gsap.to(cursorTimer, { opacity: 0, duration: 0.2 });

                gsap.to(cursorOutline, { opacity: 1, duration: 0.2 });

                gsap.set(cursorCircle, { strokeDashoffset: 164 });

            });

            gallery.addEventListener('click', (e) => {

                if (!gallery.classList.contains('expanded')) {

                    expandGallery(gallery);

                }

            });

        });

        document.querySelectorAll('.project-section').forEach(sec => {

            sec.addEventListener('mouseenter', () => {

                document.querySelectorAll('.gallery.expanded').forEach(g => {

                    if (!sec.contains(g)) {

                        g.classList.remove('expanded');

                    }

                });

            });

        });

        function expandGallery(gal) {

            document.querySelectorAll('.gallery.expanded').forEach(g => {

                if (g !== gal) g.classList.remove('expanded');

            });

           

            gal.classList.add('expanded');

           

            gsap.to(cursorTimer, { opacity: 0, duration: 0.2 });

            gsap.to(cursorOutline, { opacity: 1, duration: 0.2 });

            gsap.set(cursorCircle, { strokeDashoffset: 164 });

           

            setTimeout(() => ScrollTrigger.refresh(true), 600);

        }

        document.querySelectorAll('.hoverable, .cat-item, .menu-toggle, .lb-close, .project-link').forEach(el => {

            el.addEventListener('mouseenter', () => {

                cursorOutline.style.width = '60px';

                cursorOutline.style.height = '60px';

                cursorOutline.style.backgroundColor = 'rgba(255,145,175, 0.1)';

            });

            el.addEventListener('mouseleave', () => {

                cursorOutline.style.width = '40px';

                cursorOutline.style.height = '40px';

                cursorOutline.style.backgroundColor = 'transparent';

            });

        });

        const logoElement = document.getElementById('header-logo');

        const fonts = ['Cinzel', 'Playfair Display', 'Cormorant Garamond', 'Montserrat', 'Helvetica'];

        logoElement.innerHTML = "AYTEK JANE".split('').map(char => char === " " ? `<span class="space">&nbsp;</span>` : `<span>${char}</span>`).join('');

        const logoSpans = logoElement.querySelectorAll('span:not(.space)');

        let lastTime = 0;

        function changeLogoFont(timestamp) {

            if (timestamp - lastTime > 800) {

                const randomSpan = logoSpans[Math.floor(Math.random() * logoSpans.length)];

                const randomFont = fonts[Math.floor(Math.random() * fonts.length)];

                randomSpan.style.fontFamily = randomFont;

                lastTime = timestamp;

            }

            requestAnimationFrame(changeLogoFont);

        }

        requestAnimationFrame(changeLogoFont);

        function toggleMobileMenu() {

            const menu = document.getElementById('mobileMenu');

            const body = document.getElementById('page-body');

            menu.classList.toggle('active');

            if (menu.classList.contains('active')) { body.style.overflow = 'hidden'; } else { body.style.overflow = ''; }

        }
